### [DIVISION V]{.HLabel2}[Weighing and Measuring Machines in General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-533403 .Subheading}

#### [Definitions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533404 .Subheading}

**[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Division.

[[machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a weighing machine or
    measuring machine for use in trade. ([appareil]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[registration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[registration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes both visual indication
    and recorded representation of quantity, unit price or monetary
    value. ([enregistrement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/98-115, s. 7
- SOR/2005--130, s. 7
- [SOR/2024-183, s. 5](#1488111-1488668){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1488111-1488668.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-120-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 120"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Design, Composition and Construction]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533410 .Subheading}

**[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** A machine shall be of a
design, composition and construction that will enable it to resist such
exposure to vibration, the product measured, foreign matter, dirt,
moisture and extremes of temperature as may be encountered in its
intended service, so that wear of its moving components will be
minimized, its accuracy will be maintained, its operating parts will
continue to function properly and its means of adjustment will retain
their settings.

**[[122 and 123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122_and_123
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, SOR/98-115, s. 8\]]{.Repealed}

**[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** Any electrical or
electronic components, equipment or accessories attached to or used in
conjunction with a machine that have or can have an effect on the
accuracy of the machine shall function properly despite such
steady-state and transient variations in the frequency, voltage and
waveform of the electrical supply and disturbances in the surrounding
electromagnetic and electrostatic field as may normally exist in the
intended service.

**[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** When a machine requires
an electrical power supply, the frequency, voltage and current
requirements and any special limitations on the stability or quality of
power supply required to enable the machine to measure accurately shall
be marked on the machine.

**[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** A machine shall be
provided with means of registration that are appropriate for the class,
type or design of machine and for its intended service, installation and
use.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-297, s. 41(F)
:::

**[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** All registrations shall
be clear, definite and easily legible under conditions of normal use of
the machine.

**[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/98-115, s. 9\]]{.Repealed}

**[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** Where a machine
provides a printed record of the registration on a ticket or form, the
ticket or form shall contain a statement of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the quantity measured in the transaction or, where
  permitted or required by these Regulations or by specifications
  established by the Minister, a start and finish print such that the
  quantity measured can be calculated by subtraction;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} when the computed price is shown, the unit price for
  the commodity measured;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the name, symbol or abbreviation appropriate to the
  unit of measurement registered and, as applicable, to the unit price
  and total price; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} such further information as may be required by these
  Regulations or by specifications established by the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/93-234, s. 2
:::

**[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130 .sectionLabel}** A machine equipped with
an indicator or printer shall be capable of giving a visual or printed
indication that the device has been properly returned to start position
before any commodity is measured.

**[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** The graduations on an
analogue type of indicator or printer for a machine shall conform to the
following specifications:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in any series of graduations the lines or marks of
  corresponding graduations shall be uniform in size and character;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the width of a graduation line or mark shall not be
  greater than the width of the minimum clear interval between
  graduations;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} graduation lines or marks shall be varied in length
  or size so that they may be accurately read;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} in a series of graduations, intervals representing
  identical quantities shall not differ by a ratio greater than 1.2 to
  1;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} graduations intended to have specific values shall be
  defined by figures, words, symbols or combinations thereof uniformly
  placed with reference to the graduations and as close thereto as
  practicable, but not so positioned as to interfere with the accuracy
  of reading; and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} graduations and their defining figures, words and
  names or symbols or abbreviations for the unit of measurement shall be
  such that they will not become obliterated or illegible under
  conditions of normal use of the machine.

**[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132 .sectionLabel}** The indicating element
of an analogue type of indicator and the imprint of an analogue printer
of a machine shall conform to the following specifications:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the indicating portion of a poise or pointer and its
  relation to any associated series of graduations shall be such that
  there will be no confusion as to the quantity indicated or printed;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the indicating portion of a poise or pointer shall
  reach but shall not obscure the finest graduation with which it is
  used;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the indicating portion of a pointer and the
  graduations are in the same plane, there may be a distance of not more
  than 1.0 mm or 0.04 inch between the end of the indicating portion and
  the end of the graduations, the distance being measured along the line
  of the graduations;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the width of an indicating line or of the indicating
  portion of a pointer shall not be greater than the width of the
  narrowest graduation with which it is used; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the clearance between an indicating line or the
  indicating portion of a pointer and the reading surface of the
  graduations shall not be more than 1.5 mm or 0.06 inch unless adequate
  provision is made to prevent errors due to parallax.

**[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/98-115, s. 10\]]{.Repealed}

**[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** Mechanical digital
means of registration for a machine of the drum cyclometer or similar
type shall conform to the following specifications:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} all digits except the last shall remain centrally
  aligned;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} there shall be means to show when the last digit is
  centrally aligned;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} any graduation lines on the finest indicating element
  shall provide a decimal or binary subdivision of the quantity between
  its successive digits;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the digits representing a whole unit of measurement
  or a decimal multiple thereof shall be of uniform size, character and
  colour;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the digits representing a decimal submultiple of the
  unit of measurement shall be set apart by a decimal point or comma;
  and

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} where required by specifications for the particular
  class or type of machine, the digits representing a decimal or binary
  fraction of the unit of measurement shall be differentiated from the
  other digits by their size, character, colour or by a supplementary
  symbol.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-297, s. 41(F)
:::

**[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** Electronic digital
means of indication for a machine shall conform to the following
specifications:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} all digits shall be of the same height;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the digits representing a decimal submultiple of the
  unit shall be set apart by a decimal point or comma; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the brightness of the electronically displayed digits
  and other information shall be such that they may be easily read under
  normal conditions of use.

**[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** Associated with any
digital registration by a machine there shall be suitably located
defining words and the name, symbol or abbreviation for the unit or
units of measurement appropriate in size and such that they will not
become obliterated or illegible under normal conditions of use of the
machine.

**[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** Indicators and printers
for monetary values shall conform to the same specifications as
indicators and printers for registration of units of quantity.

#### [Performance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533457 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2005-297, s. 42(F)

\]
:::

**[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** Within such limits of
repeatability as may be required by these Regulations or by
specifications established by the Minister, a machine shall be capable
of repeating its registrations for each identical load or quantity
delivered regardless of repeated manipulations of any or all of the
elements of the machine in a manner approximating normal conditions of
use of the machine.

- **[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), where a
  machine has or is installed with two or more means of registration
  that have the same units of measurement, the registrations shall be in
  accordance with the following criteria:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of electronic digital means of
    registration, the registrations thereof shall

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} where the values of minimum increment of
      registration are the same, be in exact agreement, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} where the values of minimum increment of
      registration are not the same, be in agreement within 0.6 times
      the largest of those values of minimum increment of registration;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of mechanical digital means of
    registration, the registrations thereof shall be in agreement within
    0.6 times the largest of the values of minimum increment of
    registration;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of analogue means of registration, the
    registrations thereof shall be in agreement within 0.25 times the
    largest of the values of minimum increment of registration; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} in the case of combined digital and analogue means
    of registration, the registrations thereof shall be in agreement
    within 0.6 times the largest of the values of minimum increment of
    registration.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A means of providing analogue registration that is of
  the servo-follower type and that is installed prior to January 1, 1976
  shall be in agreement with any other analogue or digital means of
  registration connected to the same machine within twice the finest
  graduation.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/90-118, s. 19
- SOR/2005-297, s. 15
:::

**[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** The registration of
monetary value on a machine provided with means of registration for
monetary value shall be in mathematical agreement with the quantity
indication and unit price, within such monetary limits as may be allowed
by these Regulations or by specifications established by the Minister
and the machine shall have words or symbols to differentiate clearly
between the monetary value and the quantity registrations.

#### [Installation and Use]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533473 .Subheading}

**[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** A machine that has
electrical components or equipment or accessories attached to or used in
conjunction with it that have or can have an effect on the accuracy of
the machine shall be connected to an electrical supply the
characteristics of which are as specified by the manufacturer of the
machine and the electrical wiring and other electrical installation
affecting the machine shall be in accordance with the specifications of
the manufacturer and with specifications established by the Minister for
that class, type or design of machine.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-297, s. 41(F)
:::

**[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142 .sectionLabel}** The installation and
use of a machine shall be such as to ensure that it is protected from
abnormal environmental factors such as excessive dust or extremes of
temperature, vibration, electromagnetic or electrostatic fields that
could have an adverse effect on its performance.

**[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143 .sectionLabel}** A machine, other than a
scale used for prepackaging or a device described in subsection 4(2),
shall be installed so that the indications of the primary means of
indication can be clearly read by any party to the transaction for which
the machine is being used, but, if such an installation is not
practicable, a secondary means of indication shall be provided to allow
the parties to the transaction to read the indications.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-297, s. 16
- SOR/2012-28, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-143-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 143"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144 .sectionLabel}** The primary indicator
of a machine shall be so located as to allow an unobstructed view of the
load-receiving element or the delivery outlet of the machine or, if
owing to particular circumstances that is not feasible, a convenient
means shall be provided for direct communication between an observer at
the indicator and an observer at the load-receiving element or the
delivery outlet.

**[[145 to 148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145_to_148
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, SOR/98-115, s. 11\]]{.Repealed}

**[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149 .sectionLabel}** A machine shall not be
installed or used in conjunction with any supplementary means of
registration unless that means of registration conforms to the
specifications for registration set out in this Part and to the
specifications established pursuant to subsection 13(1) and section 27.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-297, s. 17
:::

### [DIVISION VI]{.HLabel2}[Automatic Weighing Machines]{.HTitleText2} {#h-533486 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533487 .Subheading}

**[[150]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150 .sectionLabel}** The following
definitions apply in this Division.

[[continuous totalizing weighing machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[continuous totalizing weighing machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
    a machine used for continuously weighing a bulk product on a
    conveyor belt, without systematic subdivision of the mass and
    without interruption of the movement of the conveyor
    belt. ([appareil de pesage totalisateur en continu]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[electronic computing machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[electronic computing machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an
    electronic machine that calculates the monetary value equal to the
    product of the weight indication multiplied by the unit
    price. ([appareil calculateur électronique]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[electronic machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[electronic machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a machine, other
    than a continuous totalizing weighing machine, that operates with
    electronic components and is equipped with a digital means of
    indication. ([appareil électronique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[known test load]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[known test load]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a load consisting of
    local standards or any other load the weight of which has been
    determined in relation to local standards. ([charge
    connue]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an automatic weighing machine
    for use in trade that weighs without the intervention of an operator
    and follows a predetermined program of automatic processes
    characteristic of the machine. ([appareil]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[registration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[registration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes both visual indication
    and printed representation of quantity, unit price or monetary
    value. ([enregistrement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[tare function]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[tare function]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in respect of a machine, means
    a process, mechanism or feature that allows it to utilize
    tare. ([fonction tare]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/90-118, s. 20
- SOR/98-115, s. 12
- SOR/2005-130, s. 8
- SOR/2012-28, s. 4
- SOR/2017-17, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-150-20120501.html "Link to previous version of section 150"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Design, Composition and Construction]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533498 .Subheading}

- **[[151]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the accuracy of registration, including the zero weight indication, of
  a portable machine is affected when it is off level, the machine shall
  be equipped with a self-locking or lockable means of levelling and a
  level indicator.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An electronic computing machine shall be equipped
  with a level indicator that is easily visible or with the tools that
  are necessary to expose the level indicator if it is not easily
  visible.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 5
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-151-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 151"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[152]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  electronic machine shall be equipped with an automatic motion detector
  to prevent the registration of weight values until the weight
  indication is stable within

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} plus or minus one times the value of the minimum
    increment of registration, if the electronic machine has a capacity
    of 2000 kg or less; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} plus or minus three times the value of the minimum
    increment of registration, if the electronic machine has a capacity
    of more than 2000 kg.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An electronic computing machine shall be equipped
  with an automatic motion detector to prevent the registration of
  weight values until the weight indication is stable within plus or
  minus one times the value of the minimum increment of registration.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The weight indication of an electronic machine shall
  be stable, in accordance with subsections (1) and (2), for at least
  0.4 of a second before registering a weight value.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The zero-setting mechanism and the zero-tracking
  mechanism circuits of an electronic machine shall be interlocked with
  the motion detector so that these mechanisms are inoperative while
  motion is being detected.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 5
- SOR/2017-17, s. 20(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-152-20120501.html "Link to previous version of section 152"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[153]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-153 .sectionLabel}** The registration of
monetary value calculated by an electronic computing machine is to be
rounded to the nearest cent as follows:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} when the digit next beyond the second decimal is less
  than 5, the second decimal is to be kept unchanged;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} when the digit next beyond the second decimal is
  greater than 5, or is 5 followed by one or more digits other than
  zeros, the second decimal is to be increased by one; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} when the digit next beyond the second decimal is 5
  and is followed by no other digits or by one or more zeros, the second
  decimal is to be kept unchanged if it is even and be increased by one
  if it is odd.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 5
- SOR/2017-17, s. 3
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-153-20120501.html "Link to previous version of section 153"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[154]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-154 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  tare function of an electronic machine shall operate only in a
  negative direction in relation to zero.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The tare function control mechanism of an electronic
  machine shall be identified with the word "TARE", the letters "TR", or
  with words or letters that convey the same meaning.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} An electronic machine may be equipped with full-scale
  tare; however, the gross weight shall not exceed the rated capacity of
  the machine in accordance with the limits set out in section 170.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The value of the minimum increment of tare of an
  electronic machine shall be the same as the value of the minimum
  increment of registration.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The use of any mechanism to convert units of
  measurement (for example, a pound/kg switch) shall be inhibited when
  the tare is entered into the memory of an electronic machine, unless
  the mechanism converts all values of weight registration when
  activated.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} The tare function of an electronic computing machine
  shall be self-cancelling so that when any net weight is added to the
  tare, the total is computed, and when the gross weight is removed from
  the machine, the weight indication returns to zero in accordance with
  section 183, unless the machine is in prepackaging mode.

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} A tare entry shall be automatically erased from an
  electronic computing machine's memory only after the completion of the
  weighing operation. The entry of another price per unit of weight, the
  use of a "clear" mechanism or any other keying sequence shall not
  affect the tare entry.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} An electronic computing machine with a tare function
  shall be equipped with a power failure interlock mechanism that
  prevents the continuation of the weighing operation when there is a
  temporary loss of power. When there is a loss of power to an
  electronic computing machine, all visual indications of the tare
  function shall extinguish and remain extinguished even when power is
  restored.

- [(9)]{.lawlabel} A negative weight value shall continue to be
  displayed on an electronic computing machine when the load-receiving
  element is empty and the tare function is in operation.

- [(10)]{.lawlabel} There shall be a visual indication on or adjacent to
  the weight display of an electronic computing machine when the tare
  function is in operation.

- [(11)]{.lawlabel} If an electronic machine, other than an electronic
  computing machine, displays only one weight value at a time, there
  shall be visual indication on or adjacent to the weight display that
  indicates that the tare function is in operation.

- [(12)]{.lawlabel} If an electronic machine, other than an electronic
  computing machine, is capable of registering gross, net or tare
  weight, the weight values shall be clearly identified.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 5
- SOR/2017-17, ss. 4(F), 20(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-154-20120501.html "Link to previous version of section 154"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[155 and 156]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155_and_156
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, SOR/98-115, s. 13\]]{.Repealed}

**[[157]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157 .sectionLabel}** A machine shall be
equipped with means to balance it at zero-load and if supplementary
material is used for that purpose it shall be so enclosed that it cannot
shift position or be readily removed or altered in such a way that the
balance condition of the machine is affected.

**[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158 .sectionLabel}** A balance ball,
balancing weight, spring adjustment, trim potentiometer or other zero
adjusting means on a machine shall be operable only by a detachable
tool, but on a machine of up to 15 kg or 30 pounds capacity, a balance
ball may be operable without a detachable tool if the design provides
for controlled friction to hold the balance ball in any set position.

- **[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  overall range of the zero-setting mechanism of an electronic machine
  that may be adjusted externally shall not exceed four per cent of the
  machine's rated capacity unless the adjustment is made using a tool
  that is not permanently affixed to the adjustment mechanism. When
  activated, that adjustment mechanism shall return the weight
  indication to zero.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The overall range of the zero-tracking mechanism of
  an electronic machine shall not exceed four per cent of the machine's
  rated capacity.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The zero-tracking mechanism of an electronic machine
  shall not be operator-controlled and the maximum weight value
  corrected at any one time by the zero-tracking mechanism shall not
  exceed 0.6 times the value of the minimum increment of registration.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 6
- SOR/2017-17, s. 20(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-159-20120501.html "Link to previous version of section 159"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160 .sectionLabel}** When an electronic
computing machine displays a weight value less than zero, the total
price indicator shall not display any value and the other registration
elements, excluding the indicating elements, shall be interlocked to
inhibit the registration of negative weight values.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 6
:::

**[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161 .sectionLabel}** The luminosity contrast
between the visual indications and the background of the indicator of an
electronic machine shall have a ratio equal to or greater than 4 to 1.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 6
:::

**[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162 .sectionLabel}** In order to allow for
the adjustment of the weight of a commodity, the weight signal of an
electronic computing machine must be free-floating until the final
weight of the commodity is obtained.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 6
:::

**[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163 .sectionLabel}** An electronic machine
shall be equipped with the means to seal the coarse zero and span
adjustment controls so that all other components and adjustments are
readily accessible without breaking the seal. The means shall be readily
observable upon examination without having to disassemble any part using
tools that are not supplied with the machine.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 6
- SOR/2014-111, s. 46
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-163-20120501.html "Link to previous version of section 163"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[164]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164 .sectionLabel}** An electronic machine
shall be equipped with a test system for verifying that each display
segment is not continuously on or off. The test system shall permit
verification of display segments without any tools or by a
self-verifying automatic system that indicates the occurrence of any
failure.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 6
:::

**[[165 to 168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165_to_168
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, SOR/98-115, s. 14\]]{.Repealed}

**[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169 .sectionLabel}** When a machine is
equipped with a ticket printer or a label printer, the printed numbers
and letters shall be of such size, character and colour that they may be
easily read under normal conditions of use.

- **[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  electronic machine, other than an electronic computing machine, shall
  not register or print any value when the load exceeds 105 per cent of
  the rated capacity of the machine.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} An electronic computing machine shall not register or
  print any value when the load exceeds the rated capacity of the
  machine by more than 10 times the value of the minimum increment of
  registration.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 7
- SOR/2017-17, ss. 5(F), 20(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-170-20120501.html "Link to previous version of section 170"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171 .sectionLabel}** A machine equipped with
an electronic digital means of registration shall be capable of giving
visual confirmation that it has been returned to zero-load within a
range equivalent to the greater of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} ¼ of the value of the minimum increment of
  registration, and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} 0.01 per cent of the capacity of the machine.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/90-118, s. 21
- SOR/2017-17, s. 20(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-171-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 171"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[172]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) to (6),
  the value of the minimum increment of registration on a machine shall
  not be greater than 10 kg, if the machine measures in kilograms, or 20
  pounds, if the machine measures in pounds, unless the capacity of the
  machine exceeds 100 000 kg or 200,000 pounds, in which case the value
  of the minimum increment of registration shall not be greater than
  20 kg or 50 pounds, respectively.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The value of the minimum increment of registration on
  a tank scale that is designed for weighing alcohol or on a hopper
  scale that is designed for weighing grain in a terminal or transfer
  elevator shall not be greater than

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 5 kg or 10 pounds, if the capacity of the scale is
    equal to or less than 100 000 kg or 200,000 pounds;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 10 kg or 20 pounds, if the capacity of the scale
    exceeds 100 000 kg or 200,000 pounds, but is equal to or less than
    200 000 kg or 400,000 pounds; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 20 kg or 50 pounds, if the capacity of the scale
    exceeds 200 000 kg or 400,000 pounds.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The value of the minimum increment of registration on
  a continuous totalizing weighing machine shall not be greater than
  100 kg or 200 pounds.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The value of the minimum increment of registration on
  an electronic computing machine shall not be greater than 10 grams or
  0.01 pound.

  an electronic computing machine, is set out in the following tables:

- [(7)]{.lawlabel} The value of the minimum increment of registration of
  an electronic machine shall be equal to the number of kilograms or
  pounds obtained by using one of the following formulas:

  ::: {#533599 .Subsection}
  1 × 10^n^, 2 × 10^n^ or 5 × 10^n^,

  where "n" represents a whole number, whether positive, negative or
  equal to zero.
  :::

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/90-118, s. 21
- SOR/2012-28, s. 8
- SOR/2017-17, s. 6
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-172-20120501.html "Link to previous version of section 172"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[173]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/98-115, s. 16\]]{.Repealed}

#### [Performance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533604 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2005-297, s. 42(F)

\]
:::

**[[174]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174 .sectionLabel}** Subject to sections 181
to 184 and 188 to 193, where a known test load set out in column I of an
item of a table to section 176 or 177 is applied to a machine for the
purpose of determining compliance with the acceptance limits of error,
the machine is within the acceptance limits of error in respect of that
known test load if the load registered by each means of registration of
the machine is not greater or less than the known test load by an amount
in excess of the amount set out in column II of that item.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/90-118, s. 22
- SOR/98-115, s. 17
:::

**[[175]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-175 .sectionLabel}** Subject to sections 181
to 184 and 188 to 193, where a known test load set out in column I of an
item of a table to section 176 or 177 is applied to a machine for the
purpose of determining compliance with in-service limits of error, the
machine is within the in-service limits of error in respect of that
known test load if the load registered by each means of registration of
the machine is not greater or less than the known test load by an amount
in excess of the amount set out in column III of that item.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/90-118, s. 22
- SOR/98-115, s. 17
:::

**[[176]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-176 .sectionLabel}** Subject to sections
188, 192 and 193, the limits of error set out in the following table
apply to all machines registering in metric units of mass:

apply to all machines registering in avoirdupois units of mass or
weight:

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/98-115, s. 19
:::

- **[[178]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  electronic machine shall perform within the applicable limits of
  error, when tested after being calibrated and zeroed, under relative
  humidity conditions between 10 per cent and 95 per cent, and under one
  of the following operating conditions:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the ambient temperature range is -10°C to +40°C,
    unless expressly specified otherwise on the approval application and
    marked on the machine;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the specified minimum temperature is lower than
    -10°C or the specified maximum temperature is more than +40°C, the
    machine shall bear markings specifying the minimum and maximum
    temperatures and shall be approved for use within this expanded
    temperature range, if Measurement Canada laboratories are equipped
    to test at those temperatures;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} when a machine meets the conditions described in
    paragraphs (a) and (b), it may be used beyond the approved minimum
    and maximum temperatures if it continues to perform within allowable
    limits of error; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the specified minimum temperature is higher than
    -10°C or the specified maximum temperature is less than +40°C, the
    machine shall bear markings specifying the minimum and maximum
    temperatures and shall only be approved for use within this reduced
    temperature range.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The difference between the specified minimum and
  maximum temperatures for an electronic machine shall not be less than

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} 5°C for machines with more than 50,000 increments
    of registration;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} 15°C for machines used to weigh precious metals and
    other commodities of comparable value; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} 30°C for machines used to weigh commodities other
    than precious metals or commodities of comparable value.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} An electronic machine shall perform within the
  applicable limits of error when tested at a constant ambient
  temperature that does not vary more than 5°C.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 9
:::

- **[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  a load remains on an electronic machine, the difference between the
  indication that is obtained at the moment that the load is applied and
  the indication that is obtained eight hours after its application
  shall not exceed the permissible acceptance limit of error for the
  applied load.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The variation on returning to zero after the removal
  of a load which has remained on an electronic machine for half an hour
  may not exceed one half the value of the minimum increment of
  registration.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The weight indication at zero or near zero on an
  electronic machine shall not vary by more than the value of the
  minimum increment of registration for a difference in ambient
  temperature of 5°C.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 9
- SOR/2017-17, ss. 7(F), 20(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-179-20120501.html "Link to previous version of section 179"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180 .sectionLabel}** An electronic machine
shall perform within the applicable limits of error when scanned at a
distance of 2 metres with

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} a 460-MHz (commercial) 4-watt hand-held communicator;
  and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} a 27-MHz (citizens' band) 4-watt hand-held
  communicator.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 9
:::

**[[181]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181 .sectionLabel}** When a machine that is
equipped with a self-indicating or semi-self-indicating means of
registration is tested by one of the following methods, the acceptance
limits of error and in-service limits of error are 1.5 times the limits
of error set out in the tables to sections 176 and 177 and set out in
sections 188 and 192:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the removal of a known test load, in the case of a
  machine that is normally used for weighing following the addition of
  load; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the addition of a known test load, in the case of a
  machine that is normally used for weighing following the subtraction
  of load.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/2017-17, s. 8
- SOR/2018-252, s. 4(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-181-20170213.html "Link to previous version of section 181"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[182]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3)
  and sections 188 and 192, the minimum limit of error when a machine is
  tested for acceptance limits of error or in-service limits of error is
  a weight equivalent to the lesser of 0.05 per cent at the capacity of
  the machine and one minimum increment of registration.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The minimum limit of error for a railway track scale
  used exclusively in weighing railway cars is the greater of 15 kg or
  30 pounds, and the value of the minimum increment of registration.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} In a strain test at a heavy duty scale, there shall
  be no minimum limit of error in respect of the known test load that is
  added to the strain load and the limit of error for a machine with
  digital registration shall not be increased as described in section
  184.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/90-118, s. 23
- SOR/93-234, s. 2(F)
- SOR/2017-17, ss. 9, 20(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-182-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 182"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[183]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183 .sectionLabel}** On removal of all load
from a machine, the means of registration of the machine shall
immediately return to zero registration within a range equivalent to the
greater of

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} ¼ of the value of the minimum increment of
  registration, and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} 0.01 per cent of the capacity of the machine.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/90-118, s. 24
- SOR/2017-17, s. 20(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-183-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 183"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184 .sectionLabel}** Subject to subsection
182(3), if a machine has digital increments of registration, the
acceptance limits of error and in-service limits of error applicable to
the digital registrations of the machine shall be increased beyond the
limits of error otherwise applicable to the digital registrations of the
machine by the equivalent of one-half the minimum increment of
registration.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/2017-17, s. 10
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-184-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 184"){.PITLink}
:::

- **[[185]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-185
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a test consisting of the
  application to a machine of the same test load up to 10 times is
  carried out under conditions approximating conditions of normal use,
  the difference between the least registered load and the greatest
  registered load shall not exceed the absolute value set out in column
  II or III, as applicable, of an item of the appropriate table to
  sections 176 and 177 that corresponds to the known test load set out
  in column I of that item that is equivalent to the greatest registered
  load.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to the testing of a
  machine in accordance with subsection 189.2(1), 190(2) or 191(1) or
  (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/90-278, s. 5
- SOR/93-234, s. 2(E)
:::

**[[186]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186 .sectionLabel}** An electronic machine
shall be able to withstand vertical forces up to 150 per cent of its
rated capacity without affecting its calibration.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 10
:::

**[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187 .sectionLabel}** An electronic machine
shall be able to operate within plus or minus 10 per cent of its voltage
rating and within plus or minus 2 per cent of its frequency rating.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 10
:::

**[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188 .sectionLabel}** Where a hopper scale or
a tank scale referred to in subsection 172(2) has a capacity equal to or
greater than 15 000 kg or 35,000 pounds,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the acceptance limits of error for that scale are the
  greater of 0.05 per cent of the known test load and the value of the
  minimum increment of registration; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the in-service limits of error for that scale are the
  greater of 0.10 per cent of the known test load and the value of the
  minimum increment of registration.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/90-118, s. 25
- SOR/2017-17, s. 20(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-188-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 188"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189 .sectionLabel}** Where a static test is
conducted on a railway track scale that is designed for in-motion
weighing, the acceptance limits of error for the scale are those
prescribed by section 174 and the in-service limits of error for the
scale are those prescribed by section 175.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/90-278, s. 6
:::

- **[[189.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In sections 189.2, 190 and 191,

  [[reference car]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[reference car]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a railway car the
      weight of which has been determined in relation to a relevant
      local standard. ([wagon de référence]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} In sections 190 and 191,

  [[net known test load]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[net known test load]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the difference
      between the static weight of a loaded reference car and the static
      weight of an empty reference car. ([charge connue
      nette]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/90-278, s. 6
:::

- **[[189.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection 182(2), where
  a railway track scale that is designed for in-motion weighing of
  uncoupled railway cars is tested dynamically, the acceptance limit of
  error and in-service limit of error for the scale are 0.15 per cent of
  the known weight of each reference car.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} The limits of error set out in subsection (1) apply
  to a test that consists of five reference cars passing over the
  railway track scale up to 10 times, under conditions approximating
  conditions of normal use.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The reference cars referred to in subsections (1)
  and (2) shall be representative of the types and weight range of the
  railway cars for which the railway track scale is intended to be used.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/90-278, s. 6
:::

- **[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[unit train]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} or
  [[UT]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a train composed of at least 10
  coupled railway cars of the same type carrying the same product to one
  consignee. (*train-bloc* or TB)

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a railway track scale that is designed for the
  in-motion weighing of unit trains is tested dynamically, the
  acceptance limit of error and in-service limit of error for the scale
  are 0.15 per cent of the sum of the net known test load of all of the
  reference cars in the train that are weighed.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The test referred to in subsection (2) shall not be
  conducted more than 10 times.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The reference cars and the length of the train used
  in the test referred to in subsection (2) shall be representative of
  the types and weight range of the railway cars and length of the
  trains for which the railway track scale is intended to be used.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s.6(F)
- SOR/90-278, s. 6
:::

- **[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a railway track scale that is
  designed for the in-motion weighing of individual coupled railway cars
  forming a train, and is used solely for determining transportation
  charges for commodities, is tested dynamically, the acceptance limits
  of error and in-service limits of error for the scale are the
  following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} at least 70 per cent of the individual weights of
    the reference cars shall be within 0.2 per cent of the known
    individual static weight of those reference cars;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} not more than five per cent of the individual
    weights of the reference cars shall differ by more than 0.5 per cent
    from the known individual static weights of those reference cars;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the weight of an individual reference car shall not
    differ by more than one per cent from the known static weight of
    that reference car.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a railway track scale referred to in subsection
  (1), used for a purpose other than determining transportation charges
  for commodities, is tested dynamically, the acceptance limit of error
  and in-service limit of error for each dynamic weighing are 0.15 per
  cent of the net known test load.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The tests referred to in subsections (1) and (2)
  shall not be conducted more than 10 times.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The reference cars and the length of the train used
  in the tests referred to in subsections (1) and (2) shall be
  representative of the types and weight range of the railway cars and
  length of the trains for which the railway track scale is intended to
  be used.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/90-278, s. 6
:::

**[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192 .sectionLabel}** When a crane scale is
used in the weighing of freight to determine freight or shipping
charges, the acceptance limit of error and in-service limit of error are
0.5 per cent of the known test load, but not less than 0.125 per cent of
the capacity of the scale.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/2017-17, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-192-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 192"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193 .sectionLabel}** The acceptance limits
of error and the in-service limits of error applicable to a continuous
totalizing weighing machine shall be 0.5 per cent of the known weight of
the material used to test the machine if the machine

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} was designed for the in-motion weighing of
  commodities to determine freight or shipping charges or is used solely
  for this purpose; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} was designed for the in-motion weighing of raw
  material such as sand, gravel, crushed rock, crude ore or other
  materials of comparable value or is used solely for this purpose.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/2005-297, s. 18
- SOR/2012-28, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-193-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 193"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[194 and 195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194_and_195
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, SOR/98-115, s. 22\]]{.Repealed}

**[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196 .sectionLabel}** The load discrimination
of a machine equipped with a self-indicating or semi-self-indicating
means of registration of either the analogue or the digital type shall
be such that for the purpose of determining compliance with the
acceptance limits of error or in-service limits of error for the machine
and at any load from zero to maximum capacity, the addition to or
removal from the load-receiving element of a weight corresponding to 1.4
times the value of the minimum increment of registration shall cause a
change in registration equal to or greater than the value of the minimum
increment of registration.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/90-118, s. 26
- SOR/2017-17, s. 12(F)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-196-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 196"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[197]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-197 .sectionLabel}** An electronic computing
machine, other than one for prepackaging use, shall clear all digital
indications of total price and price per unit of weight and shall
extinguish the tare indication either when the commodity is removed from
the load-receiving element or before another commodity is weighed and
its price is computed.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 12
:::

**[[198]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-198 .sectionLabel}** When an electronic
computing machine is in prepackaging mode there shall be a visual
indication on both the operator and customer sides of the machine,
whether by the words "for prepackaging use" or words that convey the
same meaning.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 12
:::

**[[199]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199 .sectionLabel}** The junction boxes of
an electronic machine, other than an electronic computing machine, shall
be equipped with a sealing mechanism when they contain calibration
adjustments.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 12
:::

**[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200 .sectionLabel}** When the indicator of
an electronic machine, other than an electronic computing machine, is
connected to two or more weighing locations, each of which may consist
of one or more load-receiving elements,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the indicator shall be equipped with a separate
  calibrating circuit for each weighing location, so that the adjustment
  of one circuit will not affect the calibration of any other;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} each calibrating circuit shall be marked to identify
  which weighing location it controls and that indication shall be
  clearly visible by any party to the transaction for which the machine
  is being used;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the indicator shall be equipped with an automatic
  means to clearly identify any weighing location that is in use; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} any interconnecting cable that may affect the
  calibration shall be identified to indicate the weighing location to
  which it is connected.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2012-28, s. 12
:::

#### [Installation and Use]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533751 .Subheading}

**[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/98-115, s. 23\]]{.Repealed}

**[[202]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202 .sectionLabel}** An installed machine
shall be protected against wind, rain, blowing snow, extremes of
temperature, variations in electromagnetic and electrostatic fields and
any abnormal conditions of use to ensure that the machine can measure
accurately and is not subject to premature deterioration in performance.

**[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203 .sectionLabel}** Water from any source
shall be drained away from the machine and all surfaces under the
machine, including floors of pits, shall be kept dry.

**[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204 .sectionLabel}** Before use, a portable
machine shall be placed on a level and stable supporting surface and
shall be properly levelled.

**[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed,
SOR/98-115, s. 24\]]{.Repealed}

**[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206 .sectionLabel}** A machine shall, before
use, be adjusted to register zero when there is no load on the load
receiving element, unless another mode of operation is allowed for the
class, type or design of machine by specifications established by the
Minister or by a notice of approval.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/2005-297, s. 41(F)
:::

**[[207 to 209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207_to_209
.sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, SOR/98-115, s. 25\]]{.Repealed}

### [DIVISION VII]{.HLabel2}[Linear Measuring Machines]{.HTitleText2} {#h-533761 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533762 .Subheading}

**[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210 .sectionLabel}** In this Division,

[[known test length]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[known test length]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a sample of a
    material that is representative in thickness and texture of the kind
    of material that a machine is designed to measure, the length of
    which has been determined in relation to a local
    standard; ([longueur de contrôle connue]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a linear measuring machine for
    use in trade. ([appareil]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/90-118, s. 28
:::

#### [Design, Composition and Construction]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533768 .Subheading}

**[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211 .sectionLabel}** The measuring element
on a machine shall be designed and constructed so as to reduce to a
practicable minimum any slippage of material being measured.

**[[212]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-212 .sectionLabel}** The registering element
on a machine shall be readily returnable to a definite zero indication
and means shall be provided on the machine to prevent the return of the
registering element to less than zero registration.

**[[213]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-213 .sectionLabel}** Registrations of length
and money value shall be accurate when the machine is operated at any
speed and in any manner to which it may reasonably be subjected in trade
use and when it is operated in either a forward or a backward direction.

#### [Performance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533772 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2005-297, s. 42(F)

\]
:::

**[[214]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-214 .sectionLabel}** Where a test that
consists of measuring a known test length set out in Column I of an item
of the appropriate table to sections 216 to 219 is carried out on a
machine, the machine is within the acceptance limits of error in respect
of the known test length if the length registered by the machine is

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} not less than the known test length by an amount
  greater than the amount set out in Column II of that item; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} not more than the known test length by an amount
  greater than the amount set out in Column III of that item.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/90-118, s. 29
:::

**[[215]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215 .sectionLabel}** Where a test that
consists of measuring a known test length set out in Column I of an item
of the appropriate table to sections 216 to 219 is carried out on a
machine, the machine is within the in-service limits of error in respect
of the known test length if the length registered by the machine is

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} not less than the known test length by an amount
  greater than the amount set out in Column IV of that item; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} not more than the known test length by an amount
  greater than the amount set out in Column V of that item.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/90-118, s. 29
:::

III</th>
<th class="noBorderRight noBorderBottom bottom" scope="col">Column
IV</th>
<th class="noBorderBottom bottom" scope="col">Column V</th>
</tr>

Length</th>

<th class="noBorderRight borderBottom bottom" scope="col">Over

[Previous

:::

#### [Installation and Use]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533821 .Subheading}

**[[220]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-220 .sectionLabel}** A machine shall be
firmly attached to a counter or other stable support before it is used.

**[[221]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-221 .sectionLabel}** Registration shall be
returned to zero before each use of the machine.

### [DIVISION VIII]{.HLabel2}[Area Measuring Machines]{.HTitleText2} {#h-533824 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533825 .Subheading}

**[[222]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-222 .sectionLabel}** In this Division,
[[machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an area measuring machine for use
in trade.

#### [Design, Composition and Construction]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533827 .Subheading}

**[[223]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-223 .sectionLabel}** A machine shall be
designed so that any means of adjustment that can have an effect on its
accuracy of measurement has provision for sealing.

#### [Performance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533829 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2005-297, s. 42(F)

\]
:::

**[[224]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-224 .sectionLabel}** When a machine is
tested in relation to a relevant local standard for either acceptance
limits of error or in-service limits of error by measurement of a known
area on a surface that is representative of the kind of material for
which the machine is designed, the machine is within both the acceptance
limit of error and the in-service limit of error in respect of that area
and that kind of material if the area registered does not differ from
the known area by more than 0.006 square metres or 1.5 per cent of the
known area, whichever is greater, and, in the case of a machine
measuring in Canadian units of measurement, 1/16 square foot or 1.5 per
cent of the known area, whichever is greater.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/2017-17, s. 15
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-224-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 224"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION IX]{.HLabel2}[Solid Volume Measuring Machines]{.HTitleText2} {#h-533835 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533836 .Subheading}

**[[225]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-225 .sectionLabel}** In this Division,
[[machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a machine for determining the
computed volume of boxes and other solids on the basis of three mutually
perpendicular linear measurements.

#### [Design, Composition and Construction]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533838 .Subheading}

**[[226]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-226 .sectionLabel}** Measuring faces on a
machine shall be designed so that they may be held parallel when each of
the three principal axes of the object are being measured.

**[[227]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-227 .sectionLabel}** A machine shall provide
registration of measurement in terms of either cubic decimetres or cubic
feet.

#### [Performance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533841 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2005-297, s. 42(F)

\]
:::

**[[228]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-228 .sectionLabel}** When a machine is
tested in relation to a relevant local standard for acceptance limits of
error by taking the measurement in turn of three lengths the product of
which corresponds to a computed volume set out in Column I of an item in
a table to section 230 or 231, the machine is within the acceptance
limits of error in respect of that computed volume if the amount
registered by the machine

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is not less than the computed volume by an amount in
  excess of the volume set out in Column II of that item; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} does not exceed the computed volume by an amount in
  excess of the amount set out in Column III of that item.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
:::

**[[229]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-229 .sectionLabel}** When a machine is
tested in relation to a relevant local standard for in-service limits of
error by taking the measurement in turn of three lengths the product of
which corresponds to a computed volume set out in Column I of an item in
a table to section 230 or 231, the machine is within the in-service
limits of error in respect of that computed volume if the amount
registered by the machine

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} is not less than the computed volume by an amount in
  excess of the volume set out in Column IV of that item; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} does not exceed the computed volume by an amount in
  excess of the amount set out in Column V of that item.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
:::

out in the following table apply to all solid volume machines designed

and crates:

### [DIVISION X]{.HLabel2}[Volumetric Liquid Measuring Machines in General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-533872 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533873 .Subheading}

**[[232]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-232 .sectionLabel}** In this Division,
[[machine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a volumetric liquid measuring
machine and includes a volumetric liquid meter or a measuring tank for
use in trade.

#### [Design, Composition and Construction]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533875 .Subheading}

**[[233]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-233 .sectionLabel}** A machine shall be
designed and constructed to ensure complete delivery of the measured
liquid and to prevent delivery of any unmeasured liquid.

**[[234]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-234 .sectionLabel}** All movable capacity
indicators and means of adjustment on a machine shall be sealable.

- **[[235]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-235
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Component parts of a machine the
  removal or alteration of which could have an effect on the accuracy of
  measurement of the machine shall be sealable.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply to a meter designed for
  measuring liquid food commodities.

**[[236]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-236 .sectionLabel}** Except as otherwise
authorized by specifications established by the Minister, a machine
shall register in units of volume of liquid at the temperature and
pressure of the liquid in the measuring element of the machine.

#### [Performance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533882 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2005-297, s. 42(F)

\]
:::

**[[237]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-237 .sectionLabel}** A machine may be tested
and calibrated using a liquid other than the liquid that the machine
will measure in trade if the test liquid has hydraulic properties
similar to the liquid that the machine will measure in trade or the test
liquid is used with a correction factor that has been specified by the
Minister.

#### [Installation and Use]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533886 .Subheading}

**[[238]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-238 .sectionLabel}** Where a machine is
incorporated into a measuring system, the component parts of the system,
including the liquid supply tank and any pump, piping, valve or delivery
hose, shall be installed and used so as not to cause the machine to
cease to measure accurately.

**[[239]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-239 .sectionLabel}** Unless otherwise
authorized by specifications established by the Minister, a machine
shall be installed so as to prevent the inter-mixing of liquids therein
unless it is a machine approved for that purpose.

**[[240]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-240 .sectionLabel}** When a machine is
installed in conjunction with piping other than a delivery hose or a
short jumper hose, the piping shall be of rigid material.

### [DIVISION XI]{.HLabel2}[Volumetric Liquid Meters]{.HTitleText2} {#h-533890 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533891 .Subheading}

**[[241]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-241 .sectionLabel}** In this Division,

[[meter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[meter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a volumetric liquid measuring
    machine for use in trade that is of the meter type or incorporates a
    meter; ([compteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[registration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[registration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes both visual indication
    and recorded representation of volume, unit price or monetary
    value. ([enregistrement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

#### [Design, Composition and Construction]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533895 .Subheading}

**[[242]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-242 .sectionLabel}** The rated maximum flow
of a meter shall be such that, when it is tested using refined petroleum
products with normal lubricating properties such as kerosene or fuel
oil, the meter will measure accurately without abnormal wear, and the
meter shall be de-rated if necessary for use on products with lesser
lubricating properties.

- **[[243]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-243 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  rated minimum flow of a meter designed to measure liquids other than
  liquefied gases shall be 20 per cent or less of the rated maximum flow
  of the meter.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), the rated minimum flow of
  a meter designed to measure liquefied gases shall be 33 per cent or
  less of the rated maximum flow of the meter.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} The rated minimum flow of a meter with a rated
  maximum flow of 90 L or less per minute that is designed to measure
  liquefied gases and to dispense liquefied gases to motor vehicles
  shall be 13 L or less per minute.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/90-118, s. 30
:::

**[[244]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-244 .sectionLabel}** Unless otherwise
authorized by specifications established by the Minister, a meter shall
have an integral filter or close-coupled strainer appropriate to the
type, design and size of the meter.

**[[245]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-245 .sectionLabel}** Unless otherwise
authorized by specifications established by the Minister, all meters
except slow-flow meters and slow-flow dispensers shall have an automatic
air-vapour eliminator and a meter for liquefied gases shall also have a
combination differential-pressure and back-pressure valve.

**[[246]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-246 .sectionLabel}** Any accessory for a
liquid meter that can affect the accuracy of the meter shall be
sealable.

**[[247]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-247 .sectionLabel}** [\[Revoked, SOR/93-234,
s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

**[[248]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-248 .sectionLabel}** [\[Revoked, SOR/90-118,
s. 31\]]{.Repealed}

- **[[249]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-249
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the means
  of registration on a meter shall be capable of advancement only by the
  liquid passing through the meter.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} A meter register may be designed to be reset to zero
  by mechanical advancement of the register elements if, during the
  resetting,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the elements of registration are obscured until the
    zero position is reached; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the advancing movement, once started, cannot be
    stopped until the zero position is reached.

**[[250]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-250 .sectionLabel}** The indications on any
resettable register, totalizer or ticket printer on a meter that is
designed to measure flow in both directions shall advance or reverse
according to the flow of the liquid.

**[[251]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-251 .sectionLabel}** When a meter has a
totalizer or preset register, it shall be clearly differentiated from
the delivery register.

**[[252]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-252 .sectionLabel}** A resettable register
shall have means to return the register accurately to zero, and the
money register of a computing type register shall return to zero at the
same time that the quantity register is returned to zero.

**[[253]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-253 .sectionLabel}** A computing type
register shall be designed to prevent any change of the price setting
during a delivery and so as to automatically display the unit price at
which the register is set to compute.

**[[254]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-254 .sectionLabel}** A meter with a rated
maximum flow of 350 L or less per minute that is intended to be used to
measure and deliver motor fuel to motor vehicles, watercraft and
aircraft shall be designed to be inoperable after a delivery until the
register is reset to zero.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/90-118, s. 32
- SOR/93-234, s. 2(E)
:::

**[[255]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-255 .sectionLabel}** [\[Revoked, SOR/93-234,
s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

**[[256]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-256 .sectionLabel}** Unless otherwise
authorized by specifications established by the Minister, a ticket
printer shall be interlocked so that it can

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} print only the quantity delivered; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} deliver only the quantity for which a ticket is
  printed.

**[[257]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-257 .sectionLabel}** A meter that has a
ticket printer shall be so designed that a jammed ticket can be removed
without breaking the examination seals on the means of adjusting the
registration.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/90-118, s. 33
- SOR/2014-111, s. 46
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-257-20060322.html "Link to previous version of section 257"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[258]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-258 .sectionLabel}** Where an automatic
temperature-compensated or other type of compensated register is
installed on a meter, an uncompensated register shall also be provided
that indicates the uncompensated volume measured by the meter.

**[[259]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-259 .sectionLabel}** The purchaser's
cumulative register on a key-operated self-serve meter shall be designed
so that it is readily visible to the purchaser at the time of delivery
and will permit comparison with the quantity indication of the delivery
shown by the primary indicator.

**[[260]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-260 .sectionLabel}** A meter shall have a
sealable means of adjustment that will permit the meter to be adjusted
to within 0.05 per cent of the quantity dispensed through the meter.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/90-118, s. 34
:::

#### [Performance]{.HTitleText3} {#h-533932 .Subheading}

::: HistoricalNote
\[

- SOR/2005-297, s. 42(F)

\]
:::

**[[261]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-261 .sectionLabel}** When a meter is tested
for acceptance limits of error at any flow rate between its rated
maximum flow and its rated minimum flow using a known test quantity of
liquid set out in Column I of an item in the applicable table to
sections 265 to 270, the meter is within the acceptance limits of error
in respect of that quantity if the quantity registered by the meter does
not differ from the known test quantity by an amount in excess of the
amount set out in Column II of that item.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
:::

**[[262]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-262 .sectionLabel}** When a meter is tested
for in-service limits of error at any flow rate between its rated
maximum flow and its rated minimum flow using a known test quantity of
liquid set out in Column I of an item in the applicable table to
sections 265 to 270, the meter is within the in-service limits of error
in respect of that quantity if the quantity registered by the meter does
not differ from the known test quantity by an amount in excess of the
amount set out in Column III of that item.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
:::

**[[263]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-263 .sectionLabel}** Where a meter is tested
for acceptance limits of error or in-service limits of error using three
consecutive tests conducted at any single rate of flow or at any flow
rate of the same pattern that falls between its rated maximum flow and
its rated minimum flow, the spread of the test results shall not exceed
two fifths of the applicable limit of error for the meter.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
- SOR/2005-297, s. 19
:::

**[[264]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-264 .sectionLabel}** [\[Revoked, SOR/90-118,
s. 35\]]{.Repealed}

- **[[265]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-265
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), the
  limits of error set out in the tables to subsections (2) and (3) apply
  to slow-flow dispensers incorporating either meters or self-measuring
  pumps, dispensers for motor fuel of the service station type, meters
  on refuellers for small aircraft and boats, meters on vehicle tanks
  and to all other meters for use in measuring automotive or petroleum
  liquids in retail trade and that are not otherwise mentioned in the
  tables in this Division.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The acceptance limits of error set out in Column II
  of the tables to subsections (2) and (3) apply only to tests made
  under controlled conditions such as bench tests, and for tests for
  acceptance limits of error under other conditions, the limits of error
  set out in Column III of those tables shall apply.

::: HistoricalNote
- SOR/89-570, s. 6(F)
:::

- **[[266]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-266
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (4) and (5),
  the limits of error set out in the tables to subsections (2) and (3)
  apply to all positive displacement bulk volumetric liquid meters,
  including vehicle-mounted meters, that are designed to measure volumes
  of 180 litres or more, or 40 gallons or more, of refined petroleum
  products such as gasoline and fuel oils, foodstuffs such as milk,
  liquid fertilizers of the solution type such as aqua ammonia, or other
  liquids of low viscosity.

  apply to meters, generally of a size 65 mm or 2½ inches or smaller
  that are designed to measure liquids of low viscosity:

  </tr>
  </thead>
  <tbody>
  <tr>
  <td class="noBorderRight">1</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight" scope="row">Any volume of 1 350 litres or more
  or of 300 gallons or more</td>
  <td class="noBorderRight">1/8% of known test quantity</td>
  <td>¼% of known test quantity</td>
  </tr>
  </tbody>
  </table>
  <figcaption><p>TABLE</p></figcaption>
  </figure>

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} The acceptance limits of error and in-service limits
  of error set out in the tables to subsections (2) and (3) apply to
  known test quantities equal to or greater than that delivered by the
  meter in one minute at the maximum operating rate.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} The acceptance limits of error set out in Column II
  of the tables to subsections (2) and (3) apply only to tests made
  under controlled conditions such as bench tests and, for tests for
  acceptance limits of error under other conditions, the limits of error
  set out in Column III of those tables shall apply.

::: HistoricalNote

- SOR/2018-252, s. 5

- **[[267]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-267
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (4) and (5),
  the limits of error set out in the tables to subsections (2) and (3)
  apply to all positive displacement bulk volumetric liquid meters,
  including vehicle-mounted meters, that are designed to measure volumes
  of 180 litres or more or 40 gallons or more of heavy petroleum
  products, such as bunker oils, foodstuffs such as syrups, liquid
  cattle feed, fertilizers of high specific gravities or other liquids
  of high viscosity.

- **[[269]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-269
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), the
  limits of error set out in the tables to subsections (2) and (3) apply
  to all slow-flow meters rated at 115 litres per hour or less or 25
  gallons per hour or less for use in trade in pipeline systems to
  measure fuel oil, stove oil or kerosene supplied to furnaces and other
  heating equipment, or in any other system where the meter is
  permanently connected to a point of fuel consumption.

  apply to meters designed to operate at 2.25 to 22.5 litres or more per
